# SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation Г?" 2026-10-10

- Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
  This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
- Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
  continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
- Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
  evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
  to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
- Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
  evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
  hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
  1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
  target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
  drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
  exit eligibility.
- Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
  or near-TP distance alone.
- Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
  with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
  1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
  protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
  lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
- Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
  paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
  comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
  gross profit is a net gain.
- Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
  unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
  near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
  calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
  pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
  qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
  a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
  momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
  and strictly as-of-breach evidence. Evaluate net expectancy, drawdown,
  false-hold/adverse-move rate, and include later-decline counterexamples;
  reserve an out-of-sample period before any implementation decision.
- Final protocol controls: calculate TP progress only from immutable original
  entry and original TP; exclude and report zero/negative/invalid target
  distance. Every A/B/C candidate must use identical historical data,
  evaluation periods, and fee/slippage assumptions. Candidate B must model an
  intrawindow price path sufficiently to determine which of its emergency
  floor or target was reached first; candle high/low alone is not evidence of
  that order. Pre-register the minimum sample size, required net-expectancy
  improvement, and maximum allowed drawdown deterioration before results are
  viewed, then report the qualification count for the B cohort.
- Final cohort and comparison definition: Candidate B is eligible only in the
  85% to strictly-less-than-90% original-TP-progress band, with fresh,
  quality-valid 1h `NEUTRAL`, supportive fresh/quality-valid 5m, and required
  current-frame structure/momentum evidence. Missing, stale, invalid, or
  unknown 1h/5m evidence makes the case ineligible rather than non-bearish.
  This avoids changing or competing with existing FIX-11T behaviour at 90%.
  Pre-count the eligible cohort; insufficient sample means keep collecting.
  B-versus-A is the sole primary comparison; C is secondary/exploratory and
  must have its trailing formula fixed before Replay. Verify ATR, spread, and
  tick-size availability as of breach; if historical spread is unavailable,
  use a declared fixed assumption and sensitivity analysis.
- Final breach semantics: eligibility uses `breach_tp_progress`, calculated
  from the executable price at the exact profit-lock breach, not the historic
  high-water mark. Persist/report both `max_tp_progress` and
  `breach_tp_progress`. The continuation emergency floor is distinct from the
  breached original lock; if actual executable price (including modeled
  fee/spread/slippage) already violates that floor, exit immediately. Freeze
  numerical precision, 90%-boundary precedence (FIX-11T owns 90%+), hold
  length, tolerance, floor, costs, sample threshold, and success criteria
  before inspecting results. Report confidence intervals and chronological
  out-of-sample results; a small qualifying cohort is insufficient evidence.
- Team-2 final validity controls: create and commit a numerical pre-registration
  before any outcome is viewed, recording its immutable content hash here.
  Verify available intrabar path data; where tick/price-event evidence cannot
  order TP versus emergency-floor contact, mark the case ambiguous, apply one
  conservative adverse-move-first rule consistently, and report analyses both
  with and without those cases. Compute the floor per trade as
  `max(original_lock - tolerance, fee_adjusted_breakeven)` and report when
  breakeven binds. Compare A and B as paired outcomes on the same eligible
  breach events, reporting per-event B-minus-A and the count of later declines
  avoided by A.

# Replay-versus-Production parity audit Г?" 2026-10-10

- Scope: source-level comparison of the manual Regression/Replay pipeline with
  the current paper-trading Production path. No trading code, configuration,
  database data, or Replay result was changed.
- Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
  `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
  through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
  `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
  profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
  classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
  consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
  visible, matching the documented live ordering.
- Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
  identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
  manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
  position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
  reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
  `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
  real execution latency.
- Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
  persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
  context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
  stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
  whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
  are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
  required historical evidence exists and is reported.
- Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
  `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
  does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
  protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
  are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
  should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
- Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
  parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
  Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
  Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
  price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
- Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
  and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
  evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
  to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
  scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
  release-equivalence certificate.


===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md =====
# FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule Г?" 2026-10-10

- `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
  treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
- A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
- A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
  profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
  capped at 25%.
- A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
- Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
  never authorizes an add.

# FIX-153 candle continuity rule Г?" 2026-10-10

- Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
  the data-quality gate.
- Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
  Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
  analysis, a signal, or a trade.
- A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
  generation after a repaired quality window.


===== codex-docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md =====
# 2026-10-08 Г?" FIX-153 local-candle hardening status

- Implemented: OFF-mode trader-owned candle ingestion, monotonic close tokens,
  bounded detector/lanes, canonical price evidence, direct-attempt freshness,
  REST exchange-time close validation, and controlled ADMIN drain behavior.
- Operational impact: a successful drain exits the JVM; a timeout resumes
  admission. Price protection pauses during a drain window.
- Database impact: existing FIX-153 V106/B105 schema remains required; no
  production migration or data change was run by Codex.
- Release status: NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION. Java 21, disposable-MySQL,
  crash/race, Windows lock, soak and Replay-parity validation remain open.

# 2026-10-08 Г?" FIX-153 exchange/database clock correction

- Problem: JVM/database clock skew could make a Binance-derived candle appear
  too early or too old during freshness checks.
- Solution: `ExchangeClockSynchronizer` measures Binance server time against
  database UTC and JVM time at startup and periodically; database and JVM
  callers apply their own bounded offset without HTTP inside locks.
- The clock-skew warning uses the larger absolute database or JVM offset; the
  dedicated Binance time client has a five-second connect/read bound.
- General Binance REST requests now use the configured request timeout, and
  scheduled clock sampling cannot monopolise the four-thread scheduler pool.
- Bootstrap REST reads occur before its local candle-write transaction, so
  the bounded Binance timeout does not hold a database transaction open.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 audit: analysis outcomes and protection clock

- Audit result: normal live and historical analysis persist a `TradeSignal`,
  including neutral/no-entry decisions; there is no normal analysed-without-
  durable-outcome path to reconcile.
- Corrected the post-lock direct-protection timestamp validation to use the
  JVM-to-exchange clock correction, matching initial receipt validation.
- A universal shutdown barrier remains a separate, unverified design task:
  it must cover financial mutations without treating unrelated administration
  or configuration writes as trading authority.
- Database impact: none. Production and Replay impact: execution freshness now
  follows an automatically measured exchange-aligned reference clock.
- Validation still required: unavailable-time-source, stale observation,
  positive/negative skew, and first-attempt freshness regression tests.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-154 identifier alignment

- The pending higher-timeframe BUY-validation design was renumbered from
  `FIX-146` to `FIX-154` in the project guidance and architecture record.
- It remains separate from FIX-153, unimplemented, and not approved for
  deployment. There is no database, Production, or Replay behavior change.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" Automated local-candle reconciliation direction

- Approved direction: the pending local-candle reconciler must automatically
  repair authoritative interrupted-open candles and exact absent identities in
  the active quality window. Inserted identities use `BACKFILL` provenance and
  never receive a close token or dispatch live analysis.
- No manual approval, database repair, or persisted review fence may be needed
  for pending candle/analysis reconciliation. Unverified data remains explicit
  non-executable evidence; this direction does not override legitimate
  strategy or risk-entry vetoes.
- Status: design only; no database migration, code, Production, or Replay
  behavior has changed.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" Appendix K reconciliation contract alignment

- Project guidance now records the approved pending design constraints: pure
  insert-only authoritative gap backfill; quality-window-bounded candidates;
  durable terminal outcomes; structurally isolated historical processing; a
  shared Binance REST rate-limit gate; and durable financial-effect
  reconciliation only after atomicity is proven.
- The planned universal mutation barrier is explicitly in-memory and active
  only during drain; it must not queue, delay, or persistently fence fresh
  trading outside drain. Protection for open positions during replacement is
  still an unresolved release gate.
- Status: documentation/design alignment only. No runtime behavior, schema,
  Production data, or Replay behavior was changed.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 automatic reconciliation implementation (initial slice)

- Problem: reconnect-boundary or startup candle gaps could leave local history
  invalid until a person performed a database repair.
- Solution: added V107 durable reconciliation/audit and terminal-outcome
  tables; guarded update-only authoritative recovery; pure insert-only gap
  backfill; a dedicated recovery executor; reconnect/scheduled requests;
  token-free recovery outcomes; post-ingestion late-live-close observation;
  and reconciliation participation in local drain.
- Files/classes changed: `CandleRepository`, `BinanceMarketDataClient`,
  `BinanceKlineService`, `BinanceWebSocketManager`, `LocalDrainService`,
  `LocalCandleHealth`, `SignalProcessingStore`, plus new local reconciliation,
  outcome, audit, executor-config, and V107 migration files.
- Database impact: forward migration V107 is required. Production and Replay
  impact: feature is disabled by default and has not been deployed. Universal
  mutation-barrier coverage, shared REST admission/rate-limit control,
  quarantined-effect reconciliation, MySQL crash/race tests, and Replay parity
  remain open release work.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 reconciliation outcome correction

- Corrected recovery semantics so `SKIPPED_NOT_LIVE` is recorded only when a
  recovery/backfill actually changes candle evidence; a competing live or
  existing row is not relabelled.
- `REST_NOT_FINAL` is now retryable rather than a terminal mismatch. Audit keys
  use fixed epoch-millisecond identity precision, and reconciliation is safely
  disabled by default even when configuration is absent.
- Added terminal outcomes for worker data-unavailable, historical-only,
  already-analysed, stale work, and detector expiry paths. Transient failures
  remain without a terminal outcome so retry remains possible.
- Added `LocalReconciliationAuditRepositoryTest`; targeted Java 21 tests and
  compile passed. MySQL, race/crash, rate-limit, barrier, effect-reconciler,
  and Replay validation remain open.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 reconciliation safety correction

- Recovery now requires positive loss evidence before it may finalise an open
  row: either the successor candle is already present or a reconnect was
  admitted after the candidate close. This prevents a short healthy-stream
  delay from being converted into a token-free, unanalysed close.
- REST finality/identity disagreement is retryable while the local row remains
  open. The authoritative Binance close timestamp, not the local expected
  value, is persisted after a successful exact match. A bounded first-candle
  probe identifies pre-listing identities as explicit unavailable outcomes.
- Recovery uses the short timeout client and checks drain between requests;
  late-live-close evidence now preserves its first timestamp.
- Status: Java 21 compilation passed. Dedicated reconciliation race, drain,
  MySQL, and Replay tests remain required before enabling the feature.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 deterministic reconciliation test coverage

- Added deterministic Java 21 tests for the reconciliation contract: a healthy
  late live close without successor/reconnect evidence is left untouched;
  successor evidence permits update-only repair and records a non-live outcome;
  a gap-insert race ends as `ALREADY_PRESENT` without inventing an outcome;
  a confirmed exchange gap ends as `SKIPPED_DATA_UNAVAILABLE`; and active drain
  prevents REST admission.
- Corrected the audit test to use a true terminal state (`EXPIRED`) rather than
  `REST_MISMATCH`, which is deliberately retryable.
- Extended the disposable MySQL integration test with actual affected-row
  checks for update-only authoritative repair and insert-only gap backfill.
- Files/classes changed: `LocalCandleReconciliationServiceTest`,
  `LocalReconciliationAuditRepositoryTest`, and `LocalCandleMySqlIT`.
  Database schema and runtime trading behavior are unchanged.
- Verification: the focused deterministic tests passed under Java 21. The
  MySQL integration test is compiled but cannot run until a disposable
  loopback MySQL service is supplied through the required `FIX153_TEST_MYSQL_*`
  environment variables. Reconciliation remains disabled by default.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 configuration validation correction

- Removed the duplicate top-level `local-candle` mapping from
  `application.yml` by merging the reconciliation and local-runtime settings
  into one mapping. Spring/YAML rejects duplicate keys, so this was preventing
  isolation-context startup.
- A full Java 21 `mvn test` now parses configuration correctly but is not yet
  green: two pre-existing shared-market tests fail in `Fix142DeliveryTest`
  (`fix150LegacyFlatRecoveryPreservesUncertaintyAndAllowsNextProtection` and
  `fix152RejectedMarkerContinuesWithoutProtectionReplayThenProcessesNewTick`).
  These failures are outside the reconciliation test slice and must be resolved
  or baselined before release certification.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 shared Binance REST admission gate

- Added `BinanceRestAdmissionGate`, a process-wide cooldown for Binance HTTP
  429 and 418 replies. All `BinanceMarketDataClient` REST paths now pass through
  it, including server-time, bootstrap klines, reconciliation klines, depth,
  and exchange-info reads.
- The gate rejects a new REST request during the exchange cooldown; it does not
  queue work, take database locks, disable WebSocket ingestion, or pause price
  protection. Individual callers retain their own retry behavior.
- Added deterministic tests for cooldown expiry and for preserving the longer
  of overlapping cooldowns. Focused Java 21 tests pass.
- Files changed: `BinanceMarketDataClient`, new `BinanceRestAdmissionGate` and
  `BinanceRestAdmissionGateTest`. No database, Production, or Replay state is
  changed. Reconciliation remains disabled pending the remaining release gates.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation design

- Added the implementation-ready design record for the pending higher-timeframe
  BUY validation change. It specifies data-quality persistence, time-bounded
  evidence selection, trust bands, hard-veto/recovery conditions, immutable
  audit evidence, preservation of legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO`, and Replay parity.
- Clarified that the legacy `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md` readiness
  document is not the FIX-154 implementation source.
- Design only: no Java, migration, database, Production, or Replay behavior
  changed.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-154 trusted-bearish policy decision

- Recorded the approved policy: HARD trusted bearish 1h vetoes; unknown bearish
  1h remains blocked; only a trusted SOFT bearish 1h may permit an otherwise
  valid first entry, capped at 25%, with all add paths blocked while SOFT.
- Defined legacy 5m veto precedence, append-only/best-effort audit behavior,
  post-deployment unknown-quality behavior, health counters, and distinct BNB
  #150 historical/replay regression cases.
- Read-only 30-day local evidence supports the fixed confidence split:
  `STRONG_SELL` has 203 rows at >=70 and 192 below 70.
- Design only: no Java, migration, or runtime behavior changed.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-154 Round 5 release-build decision

- The FIX-154 build must include the renumbered FIX-153 V108 migration because
  ordinary signal processing depends on `candle_analysis_outcome`; reconciliation
  runtime remains disabled.
- Appended safeguards: FIX-154 does not use `signal_mode`; effective context is
  immutable/transient and cannot mutate stored signals; progressive Soft audit
  evidence is best-effort and cannot influence an add decision.
- Design only: no Java, migration, or runtime behavior changed.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" V108 outcome-table startup guard

- Renumbered the unreleased FIX-153 reconciliation migration from V107 to V108
  to reserve V107 for the pending FIX-154 migration. No applied migration was
  edited.
- Added `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`: startup now fails clearly if
  `candle_analysis_outcome` is absent even though outcome-writing runtime code
  is deployed. Added focused H2 coverage for the missing-table and present-table
  paths; Java 21 focused tests pass.
- V108 ships with FIX-154 because current live signal processing writes the
  outcome table. Reconciliation remains disabled.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-154 implementation start: V107

- Confirmed read-only Flyway history: neither V107 nor V108 is applied to the
  configured local trader database.
- Added V107 as the first FIX-154 implementation artifact. It adds nullable
  `trade_signal.data_quality_valid` and the append-only execution assessment
  audit table. It deliberately has no `signal_mode` dependency or unique
  execution-audit key.
- No execution behavior has changed yet; BUY assessment/service integration is
  pending implementation and review.

# 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-154 deployment-order and cap correction

- FIX-154 is now scheduled before remaining FIX-153 runtime work and no longer
  depends on `signal_mode`. Its V107 migration is reserved from the actual
  local Flyway history; the unreleased FIX-153 V107 must become V108 before
  implementation.
- Defined assessment-level `maxInitialPercent=25` for SOFT outcomes and required
  every self-sizing caller to apply it. Kept progressive raw-1h support checks
  unchanged; they already block add stages for bearish 1h evidence.
- Corrected unknown-bearish/5m-veto priority and added SOFT counters.
- Design only: no Java, migration, or runtime behavior changed.
# 2026-10-10 Г?" FIX-154 trusted higher-timeframe BUY assessment (implementation)

- **Problem:** every bearish 1h row had equal veto authority, including
  historical rows with unknown data-quality provenance; there was no durable
  explanation of the effective 5m/1h BUY context.
- **Root cause:** BUY validation and consolidation read raw `TradeSignal`
  decisions directly, with no assessment of quality or bearish strength.
- **Solution:** added `data_quality_valid` persistence to `TradeSignal` and
  `AnalysisService`; introduced `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService`; wired it
  into direct BUY, accumulated-evidence authority, consolidation and
  observational progressive-add evidence. The SOFT 1h result is capped at 25%
  and uses immutable effective decisions; HARD and unknown bearish evidence
  vetoes. `V107` creates `data_quality_valid` and append-only
  `buy_htf_assessment_audit`.
- **Files/classes:** `TradeSignal`, `AnalysisService`,
  `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService`, `TradeExecutionValidationService`,
  `OpportunityConsolidationService`, `ExecutionIntelligenceService`,
  `BuyHtfAssessmentAuditStore`, `V107__fix_154_trusted_higher_timeframe_buy.sql`,
  and focused tests.
- **Database impact:** new nullable `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`; no
  backfill. Historical `NULL` stays unknown. New append-only audit table.
- **Production / Replay impact:** the same shared validation service is used
  for both. Audit failure is explicitly non-blocking. V108 ships because live
  runtime outcome writers require its schema; reconciliation remains disabled.
- **Validation status:** focused Maven tests could not run in this shell because
  the active JDK reports `release version 21 not supported`; execute with the
  project Java 21 toolchain before release.

## Validation correction Г?" 2026-10-10

The shell was switched to the installed Java 21 toolchain
(`C:\Program Files\Java\jdk-21`). Focused Maven verification then passed:
`HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentServiceTest`,
`TradeExecutionValidationServiceTest`, and
`OpportunityConsolidationServiceTest`.

## FIX-154 review-round correction Г?" 2026-10-10

- **Replay parity:** V107 now brings `trade_signal_test` forward with the
  V97 BTC freshness fields, V106 `signal_mode`, and FIX-154
  `data_quality_valid`, matching the reflection-based Replay writer.
- **Audit isolation:** audit records are snapshot and submitted to a bounded,
  one-thread executor. Replay never writes a Production audit row, and audit
  saturation/failure only increments an in-memory metric.
- **Evidence accuracy:** audit candle close times are derived from candle open
  time plus the interval, hard-confirmation is persisted, and both legacy veto
  codes are retained.
- **Startup safety:** outcome-schema verification now occurs at bean creation;
  FIX-154 verifies its required production and replay columns before runtime
  admission.
- **Validation:** Java 21 focused tests passed, including the higher-timeframe
  matrix, validation, consolidation and early outcome-schema verifier tests.

## FIX-154 closure work Г?" 2026-10-10

- Added explicit SOFT-policy tests for conservative/direct/context/consolidated
  sizing, neutral-5m non-escalation, untrusted non-bearish context, quality
  boundaries, and Replay audit suppression.
- Extended the dedicated disposable MySQL migration test to assert V107 and
  Replay-table/FIX-154 audit schema creation when the MySQL release test stage
  is run.
- Added read-only FIX-154 health counters at `/api/fix154/health`.
- The MySQL test remains an external release-stage proof: it cannot run in this
  session without the designated disposable MySQL environment variables.

and the remaining external MySQL/release proof.

## FIX-153 automatic reconciliation enabled Г?" 2026-10-10

- Problem: a missing historical candle left current candle ingestion healthy
  but blocked later signal generation for the affected lane.
- Root cause: the data-quality gate correctly rejects incomplete windows; the
  recovery component was present but disabled.
- Solution: enabled OFF-mode token-free reconciliation, kept its one-worker
  executor as the default, and added deterministic tests for reconnect
  evidence, bad local close-time replacement, and per-pass REST budgeting.
- Files changed: `application.yml`, `LocalCandleReconciliationConfig`, and
  `LocalCandleReconciliationServiceTest`.
- Database impact: uses existing V108 audit/outcome tables; no data rewrite or
  backfill was executed by this change.
- Production/Replay impact: recovery is non-executable and has no Replay
  authority. Targeted Java 21 reconciliation tests passed; real-MySQL release
  tests still require the disposable test environment.

## Flyway V107 checksum recovery Г?" 2026-10-10

- Problem: startup failed because the V107 file was changed after that version
  had been applied to the local database.
- Solution: restored the exact applied V107 content and checksum
  (`-501147284`). Moved the later Replay-table columns and audit
  `hard_confirmation_present` column into forward-only V109.
- Verification: Flyway's checksum calculator matches the stored V107 value;
  `Fix154MigrationContractTest` and `LocalCandleReconciliationServiceTest`
  pass on Java 21.
# 2026-10-15 Г?" FIX-156 Replay-only foundation (in progress)

- Problem: the existing Replay path reads live wallet settings and exchange
  constraints, so it cannot support an isolated historical parity claim.
- Solution started: added Replay-local policy-profile/access-guard classes and
  additive V110 tables (`replay_policy_profile`, `replay_fixture`,
  `replay_run_output`, `replay_decision_trace`). The guard fails closed for
  account/unregistered table names.
- Files/classes: `com.crypto.regression.replay.*`, V110, and Replay-only unit

- Database impact: additive Replay-owned schema only; no migration was applied.
- Production impact: none. Replay service rewiring, fixture use, and parity
  comparison are still incomplete; this is not deployable or final.

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md =====
   1: # Crypto AI Trader Г?" Architecture
   2: 
   3: Status labels used in this document:
   4: 
   5: - **Current** Г?" present in the repository today.
   6: - **Pending** Г?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
   7: - **Legacy/shared** Г?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
   8:   while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.
   9: 
  10: ## Current release status
  11: 
  12: **FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
  13: It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
  14: in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
  15: two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.
  16: 
  17: FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
  18: and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.
  19: 
  20: **FIX-156 is in progress and Replay-only.** A foundation exists for immutable
  21: Replay policy profiles, Replay-owned evidence/output tables, and a fail-closed
  22: Replay SQL allow-list. The existing Replay service has not yet been rewired
  23: away from its live wallet/Binance/shared-service dependencies, so FIX-156 is
  24: not complete or deployable.
  25: 
  26: ## System purpose
  27: 
  28: The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
  29: market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
  30: and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
  31: Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.
  32: 
  33: ## Runtime and data ownership
  34: 
  35: | Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
  36: |---|---|---|
  37: | Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
  38: | Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
  39: | Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
  40: | Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
  41: | Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |
  42: 
  43: ## Current primary flow
  44: 
  45: ```text
  46: Binance WebSocket kline
  47:   -> BinanceKlineService
  48:   -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
  49:       -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
  50:       -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
  51:       -> observer transaction
  52:       -> close-dispatch transaction
  53:   -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
  54:   -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
  55:   -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
  56:   -> TechnicalIndicatorService
  57:   -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
  58:   -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
  59:   -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
  60: ```
  61: 
  62: `KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
  63: close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
  64: run on the Binance ingestion thread.
  65: 
  66: ## Current core persistence
  67: 
  68: | Table/domain | Purpose |
  69: |---|---|
  70: | `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
  71: | `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
  72: | `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
  73: | `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
  74: | `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
  75: | Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |
  76: 
  77: ## Legacy/shared-market path
  78: 
  79: **Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**
  80: 
  81: The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
  82: `SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
  83: tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
  84: and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
  85: dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.
  86: 
  87: The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
  88: and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
  89: trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.
  90: 
  91: `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
  92: that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
  93: bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
  94: not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.
  95: 
  96: ## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)
  97: 
  98: The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
  99: ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
 100: path must never run together.
 101: 
 102: Implemented safeguards:
 103: 
 104: - Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
 105:   closed candle.
 106: - A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
 107:   result plus in-process admission coordination.
 108: - A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
 109:   the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
 110: - Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
 111:   their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
 112: - Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
 113:   pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
 114: - Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
 115: - OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
 116:   query retired shared-market tables.
 117: - Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
 118:   runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
 119:   provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
 120:   automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
 121:   candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
 122:   the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
 123: - Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
 124:   boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
 125:   not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
 126:   analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
 127:   repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
 128:   gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
 129:   provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
 130:   and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
 131:   case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
 132:   not proof that the gap is closed.
 133: - Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
 134:   duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
 135:   REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
 136:   terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
 137:   a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
 138:   failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
 139: - The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
 140:   not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
 141:   queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
 142:   protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
 143: - Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
 144:   V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
 145:   pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
 146:   request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
 147:   unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
 148: - Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
 149:   protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
 150: - Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
 151:   database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
 152:   separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
 153:   HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
 154:   General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
 155:   clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
 156:   Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
 157:   transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
 158:   REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
 159: - An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
 160:   quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
 161:   the controlled drain window.
 162: 
 163: ## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine
 164: 
 165: For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:
 166: 
 167: ```text
 168: PENDING
 169:   -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
 170:   -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
 171:   -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
 172: ```
 173: 
 174: Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
 175: Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
 176: direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.
 177: 
 178: The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
 179: protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
 180: Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
 181: `REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
 182: live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.
 183: 
 184: The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
 185: operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.
 186: 
 187: ## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation
 188: 
 189: FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.
 190: 
 191: Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
 192: only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
 193: strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
 194: evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
 195: remains unchanged.
 196: 
 197: The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
 198: `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
 199: a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
 200: rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.
 201: 
 202: The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
 203: It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
 204: the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.
 205: 
 206: ## Deployment posture
 207: 
 208: Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
 209: implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.
 210: 
 211: Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
 212: real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
 213: tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
 214: runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
 215: two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
 216: enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
 217: compile the Java 21 project.
 218: 
 219: No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
 220: without explicit user approval.
 221: 
 222: ## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)
 223: 
 224: The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
 225: core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
 226: outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
 227: boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
 228: that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.
 229: 
 230: The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
 231: but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
 232: run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
 233: can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
 234: barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
 235: atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
 236: and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
 237: must stay `false`.
 238: 
 239: The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md =====
   1: # FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule Г?" 2026-10-10
   2: 
   3: - `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
   4:   treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
   5: - A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
   6: - A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
   7:   profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
   8:   capped at 25%.
   9: - A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
  10: - Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
  11:   never authorizes an add.
  12: 
  13: # FIX-153 candle continuity rule Г?" 2026-10-10
  14: 
  15: - Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
  16:   the data-quality gate.
  17: - Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
  18:   Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
  19:   analysis, a signal, or a trade.
  20: - A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
  21:   generation after a repaired quality window.
===== codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md =====
   1: # Local candle coverage check Г?" 2026-10-09
   2: 
   3: - Scope: read-only check of all 21 enabled symbols in `crypto_ai.candle`, over the two-hour window ending at the database UTC clock.
   4: - Current state: all 63 enabled symbol/interval lanes (1m, 5m, 1h) have closed-candle records and current live ingestion. The newest 1m candle was 54 seconds old at the check.
   5: - Continuity: 5m and 1h had no internal gaps in the inspected window. Twelve symbols had one missing 1m candle: the interval from `2026-10-08T22:18:00Z` to `2026-10-08T22:19:00Z` (KSA `01:18` to `01:19`). This coincides with the local application startup period; the check establishes the gap but does not prove its cause.
   6: - Impact: current live signals are being generated, but the missing historical 1m candles should be reconciled before relying on uninterrupted historical indicator lineage for that startup window. No database data or code was changed by this check.
   7: 
   8: # Signal-staleness root cause Г?" 2026-10-09
   9: 
  10: - System Health CRITICAL signal-staleness alerts were confirmed as accurate for 1m and 5m analysis, not a dashboard timezone issue. At the check, candles continued through `2026-10-09T06:43:00Z` (1m) and `06:35:00Z` (5m), while the newest live signals were `04:34:03Z` (1m) and `04:30:07Z` (5m).
  11: - The most recent 210-candle data-quality window has a synchronized gap for every enabled symbol: missing 1m open `2026-10-09T04:34:00Z` (KSA 07:34) and missing 5m open `2026-10-09T04:30:00Z` (KSA 07:30).
  12: - Cause confirmed from the WebSocket lifecycle log: generation 1 was revoked at `2026-10-09T04:35:00.133951100Z`; generation 2 was admitted at `04:35:01.369103400Z` (1,236 ms interruption). The affected 1m and 5m candles had both closed at the reconnect boundary. Binance did not replay their final closed-kline events, leaving the already-created local rows open rather than finalized.
  13: - `CandleDataQualityService` is configured with `minimum-candles: 210` and `maximum-missing-candles: 0`. Therefore one missing candle makes the entire relevant analysis window invalid. Runtime logs confirm `DATA_QUALITY_BLOCKED` with `Missing 1 candle(s) in the analysis window` for current 1m and 5m worker attempts.
  14: - 1h has no comparable gap and produced a live signal at `2026-10-09T06:00:03Z` (KSA 09:00).
  15: - No repair or configuration change was made. Correct remediation must restore authoritative missing candles rather than relaxing the zero-missing data-quality safety rule.
  16: 
  17: ## Bounded local repair completed
  18: 
  19: - Authorized local repair finalized the 42 interrupted `RUNTIME` rows using Binance REST candles for the exact identities above: 21 x 1m and 21 x 5m.
  20: - Each response was validated for the expected open and close timestamp before one transaction finalized only a row where `closed=0`. Existing closed candles, provenance, and close-transition tokens were not changed; no close-dispatch, analysis, signal, or trade was initiated by the repair.
  21: - Verification: all 1m/5m 210-candle quality windows had zero gaps after the repair. The next live close produced new LIVE signals at `2026-10-09T07:30:07Z` for both 1m and 5m.
  22: 
  23: # ETHUSDT paper-trade 59 investigation Г?" 2026-10-09
  24: 
  25: - Scope: local `crypto_ai` paper trade `wallet_trade.id=59` for `ETHUSDT`; all database reads were bounded and read-only. Stored UTC timestamps are shown below with their KSA (`UTC+03:00`) interpretation.
  26: - Entry: signal `trade_signal.id=69226` was generated at `2026-10-09T07:33:00.233691Z` (10:33:00.234 KSA) from the 1m candle opened at 10:32 KSA. It was a `BUY`, score 79, confidence 70, entry allowed, at 2502.77. The trade executed at `07:33:01.654548Z` (10:33:01.655 KSA), 1.42 seconds after the signal; this is not an execution-latency issue.
  27: - Exit: `wallet_trade.id=60` closed the position at 2504.30 at `07:34:34.196100Z` (10:34:34.196 KSA) with `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. The managed position recorded a high of 2505.80 and an activated profit-lock price of 2504.429502243252. The realized paper gain was about 0.02445 USDT before fees (0.015982291620 ETH x 1.53 USDT).
  28: - Market path: ETH rose from 2485.88 at 07:54 KSA to 2502.77 at entry (+16.89, +0.68%). It reached 2505.56 at 10:25 KSA before the entry; entry was 2.79 (0.11%) below that high. The post-entry high was 2505.93 at 10:34 KSA, only 3.16 (0.13%) above entry, then the candle retraced. This supports the user's observation that entry occurred late in the observed move.
  29: - Primary cause: the local candle-quality interruption stopped 1m ETH signals after 07:34 KSA. There were no ETH 1m signals from 07:34 to 10:26 KSA, the period containing most of the rise. The interrupted 1m and 5m rows were repaired at about 10:28 KSA, so analysis resumed only near the later consolidation/breakout attempt. This is a confirmed data-availability gap, not proof that the strategy would have bought near 07:54 KSA.
  30: - Immediate signal sequence after recovery: at 10:30 KSA the 5m signal was a BUY but blocked by `RANGE_ENTRY_LOCATION`; at 10:31 KSA the 1m raw BUY was vetoed by a bearish BTC context; at 10:33 KSA BTC context became neutral and the 1m trend-following BUY was allowed. The entry signal itself explicitly recorded mixed higher-timeframe context (5m BUY, 1h NEUTRAL) and a weakening but ultra-close ask wall before its target.

It was nevertheless a late entry relative to the full 07:54Г?"10:33 KSA advance because the system lacked signal coverage during the main move. The data gap is an operational defect already covered by the local-candle recovery deployment gate.   86: # SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation Г?" 2026-10-10
  87: 
  88: - Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
  89:   This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
  90: - Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
  91:   continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
  92: - Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
  93:   evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
  94:   to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
  95: - Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
  96:   evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
  97:   hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
  98:   1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
  99:   target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
 100:   drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
 101:   exit eligibility.
 102: - Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
 103:   or near-TP distance alone.
 104: - Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
 105:   with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
 106:   1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
 107:   protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
 108:   lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
 109: - Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
 110:   paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
 111:   comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
 112:   gross profit is a net gain.
 113: - Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
 114:   unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
 115:   near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
 116:   calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
 117:   pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
 118:   qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
 119:   a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
 120:   momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
 121:   and strictly as-of-breach evidence. Evaluate net expectancy, drawdown,
 122:   false-hold/adverse-move rate, and include later-decline counterexamples;
 123:   reserve an out-of-sample period before any implementation decision.
 124: - Final protocol controls: calculate TP progress only from immutable original
 125:   entry and original TP; exclude and report zero/negative/invalid target
 126:   distance. Every A/B/C candidate must use identical historical data,
 127:   evaluation periods, and fee/slippage assumptions. Candidate B must model an
 128:   intrawindow price path sufficiently to determine which of its emergency
 129:   floor or target was reached first; candle high/low alone is not evidence of
 130:   that order. Pre-register the minimum sample size, required net-expectancy
 131:   improvement, and maximum allowed drawdown deterioration before results are
 132:   viewed, then report the qualification count for the B cohort.
 133: - Final cohort and comparison definition: Candidate B is eligible only in the
 134:   85% to strictly-less-than-90% original-TP-progress band, with fresh,
 135:   quality-valid 1h `NEUTRAL`, supportive fresh/quality-valid 5m, and required
 136:   current-frame structure/momentum evidence. Missing, stale, invalid, or
 137:   unknown 1h/5m evidence makes the case ineligible rather than non-bearish.
 138:   This avoids changing or competing with existing FIX-11T behaviour at 90%.
 139:   Pre-count the eligible cohort; insufficient sample means keep collecting.
 140:   B-versus-A is the sole primary comparison; C is secondary/exploratory and
 141:   must have its trailing formula fixed before Replay. Verify ATR, spread, and
 142:   tick-size availability as of breach; if historical spread is unavailable,
 143:   use a declared fixed assumption and sensitivity analysis.
 144: - Final breach semantics: eligibility uses `breach_tp_progress`, calculated
 145:   from the executable price at the exact profit-lock breach, not the historic
 146:   high-water mark. Persist/report both `max_tp_progress` and
 147:   `breach_tp_progress`. The continuation emergency floor is distinct from the
 148:   breached original lock; if actual executable price (including modeled
 149:   fee/spread/slippage) already violates that floor, exit immediately. Freeze
 150:   numerical precision, 90%-boundary precedence (FIX-11T owns 90%+), hold
 151:   length, tolerance, floor, costs, sample threshold, and success criteria
 152:   before inspecting results. Report confidence intervals and chronological
 153:   out-of-sample results; a small qualifying cohort is insufficient evidence.
 154: - Team-2 final validity controls: create and commit a numerical pre-registration
 155:   before any outcome is viewed, recording its immutable content hash here.
 156:   Verify available intrabar path data; where tick/price-event evidence cannot
 157:   order TP versus emergency-floor contact, mark the case ambiguous, apply one
 158:   conservative adverse-move-first rule consistently, and report analyses both
 159:   with and without those cases. Compute the floor per trade as
 160:   `max(original_lock - tolerance, fee_adjusted_breakeven)` and report when
 161:   breakeven binds. Compare A and B as paired outcomes on the same eligible
 162:   breach events, reporting per-event B-minus-A and the count of later declines
 163:   avoided by A.

 172:   through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through

 174:   profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
 175:   classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
 176:   consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
 177:   visible, matching the documented live ordering.
 178: - Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
 179:   identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
 180:   manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
 181:   position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
 182:   reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
 183:   `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
 184:   real execution latency.
 185: - Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
 186:   persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
 187:   context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
 188:   stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
 189:   whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
 190:   are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
 191:   required historical evidence exists and is reported.
 192: - Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
 193:   `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It

   1: # FIX-156 - Replay/Production Parity: Consolidated Preparation and Final Pre-Implementation Review
   2: 
   3: **Revision:** Consolidated preparation, E01/E02 draft evidence, and final static code review, 2026-10-15  
   4: **Status:** NOT APPROVED FOR IMPLEMENTATION  
   5: **Prepared:** 2026-10-11 KSA  
   6: **Source design:** owner-provided `FIX-156-RPR-0.9.12-DRAFT.md`  
   7: **RPR-0.9.12 SHA-256:** `ddfd5f64a95f6b55ed39e8bffa7b4c93bd65f4e6dd3b23d8bf821500de722b24`  
   8: **Owner decision O-8:** RPR-0.9.12 incorporates option (a), the narrowly scoped `fix122_evaluation` exception in its Section 2.1 and in D48/R156-05/T156-09b.  
   9: **This document review SHA-256:** Pending final freeze; calculate and record it in the review request only after the last content edit.

  11: 
  12: ## 1. Fixed scope and earned results
  13: 
  14: FIX-156 changes **Replay only**. It does not change Production source,
  15: behaviour, database writes, account state, schedulers, HTTP traffic, or
  16: latency. Replay is a manual single-symbol research tool with fixed notional
  17: output. It is not a wallet, balance, cash, reserve, budget, portfolio, or
  18: historical-fill simulator.
  19: 
  20: A run has two independent earned certification dimensions:
  21: 
  22: - `ANALYSIS_PARITY`: a qualified Production signal/outcome is reproduced
  23:   using as-of evidence.
  24: - `LIFECYCLE_PARITY`: a qualified recorded-entry lifecycle passes the
  25:   separate lifecycle metric and critical-safety gate.
  26: 
  27: A run may earn neither, one, or both dimensions. `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`
  28: and `FAILED_REPLAY` are terminal states which prevent either claim.
  29: `FAILED_REPLAY` is only for a technical error, cancellation, or failed
  30: staging. A business mismatch is published as parity-not-earned with its first
  31: divergence; it is not a technical failure.
  32: 
  33: No result may claim wallet, account, exact-fill, live-scheduling, MySQL-lock,
  34: or Binance-availability parity.
  35: 
  36: ## 2. Decided owner rules
  37: 
  38: | Decision | Implementable rule |
  39: | --- | --- |
  40: | O-1 | Replay never calls `wallet_settings` or shared `validateBuy`. A Replay-local BUY mirror reads only a frozen policy profile. |
  41: | O-2 | Signal and lifecycle parity are separate certification dimensions. |
  42: | O-3 | Every class reachable from a Replay request or worker is inside the boundary. |
  43: | O-4 | The 99% result is separate per metric, based on a frozen lower confidence bound, coverage floor, and critical-safety gate. |
  44: | O-5 | A lifecycle fixture carries the full recorded timeline, not entry only. |
  45: | O-6 | No separate Replay database identity/datasource is introduced. |
  46: | O-7 | No shared-datasource SQL guard is introduced. Replay safety is application/test enforced, not a database-permission claim. |
  47: | O-8 / D48 | Replay may use `fix122_evaluation` only through the exact run-owned and display-only operations in Section 4.2a. This exception never supplies decision, wallet, account, fill, or Production-trading authority. |

  83: 
  84: ### 4.2a `fix122_evaluation` transitional shared-table exception and delete policy
  85: 
  86: `fix122_evaluation` is a shared diagnostics/evidence table which Production
  87: also writes. The approved D48 boundary exception is deliberately narrow: it
  88: supports run-owned Replay diagnostic evidence plus one read-only Production
  89: diagnostic display. It cannot participate in a Replay decision, parity
  90: classification, qualification, entry, exit, sizing, or lifecycle transition.
  91: 
  92: The permitted run-owned operations are:
  93: 
  94: ```sql
  95: DELETE FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id IS NOT NULL
  96: ```
  97: 
  98: For the eventual baseline, this is an explicit table allow-list exception, not
  99: an untracked boundary violation. The allow-list and static reachability tests
 100: must permit only the following exact operations:
 101: 
 102: - read, insert, and query rows whose `test_run_id` identifies the active
 103:   Replay run;
 104: - the scoped purge above, never an unqualified delete and never a delete of a
 105:   row where `test_run_id IS NULL`;
 106: - the existing read-only display query `SELECT * FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE
 107:   test_run_id IS NULL AND symbol=? AND evaluated_at>=? AND evaluated_at<=?
 108:   ORDER BY id`. It may populate diagnostics display only and must not feed a
 109:   decision, parity result, fixture, or any mutable state;
 110: - no other select, insert, update, delete, repository, JDBC template, or
 111:   indirect access to this table.
 112: 
 113: The direct and background-task tests must fail the build for any operation
 114: outside this exact policy. Any other shared-table access remains forbidden.
 115: D48 remains open until the implemented policy and its direct/background-task
 116: tests prove these limits. RPR-0.9.12 adopts this owner decision. It remains a
 117: draft and is not an implementation authorization.
 118:

 132: ## 6. Mandatory logging registry
 133: 
 134: There is one registry only. Every Replay log starts
 135: `[FIX-156][REPLAY]`, includes `runId`, `profileHash`, `baselineHash`,
 136: `symbol`, and when present `fixtureId`, `signalId`, and `priceEventId`.
 137: Every time is displayed as stored UTC **and** KSA (`+03:00`). No credential,
 138: token, account number, raw SQL parameter, or unbounded payload is logged.
 139: 
 140: The only event IDs are:
 141: 
 142: - `RUN_REQUESTED`, `RUN_QUALIFIED`, `RUN_REFUSED`, `RUN_FAILED`, `RUN_PROMOTED`;
 143: - `FIXTURE_LOADED`;
 144: - `PRICE_SPAN`;
 145: - `DECISION`;
 146: - `FIRST_DIVERGENCE`;
 147: - `FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY`; and
 148: - `POLICY_FINGERPRINT`.
 149: 
 150: Event payloads include the RPR-0.9.10 required fields: reason/evidence counts,
 151: fixture hash/count, price-span boundaries/gap/refusal, decision path and
 152: Section-3.3.1 step, input event IDs, expected/actual first divergence, attempted
 153: class/method/table/URL, and policy source/configuration/profile identity.
 154: Volume is bounded by E05. Tests assert one correctly correlated event per
 155: occurrence.
 156: 
 157: A critical-safety mismatch is `ERROR` and **parity-not-earned**. Its mismatch
 158: certificate and first divergence are published. It never becomes
 159: `FAILED_REPLAY` merely because the result disagrees. `FAILED_REPLAY` means
 160: technical error/cancellation/failed staging and discards staged artifacts.
 161:

 193: ## 8. Completion criteria
 194: 
 195: Before any parity claim, actual changes must reconcile to the approved registry;
 196: all required tests and E01-E10 evidence must exist; the qualified/excluded/
 197: refused/non-comparable/censored buckets must be reported by cause and exit
 198: class; and the frozen coverage/count/lower-bound requirements must pass
 199: separately for both dimensions.
 200: 
 201: There must be **zero unexplained critical-safety mismatches** in stop-loss,
 202: profit-lock, take-profit, near-TP ownership and duplicate-exit classes. A
 203: qualified cohort may earn its applicable `ANALYSIS_PARITY` and/or
 204: `LIFECYCLE_PARITY` dimension only. It never earns account or exact-fill parity.

 215: The current Replay path is not inside the approved boundary yet:

 223:   `StopLossEvidenceStore` can fall through to Production-account reads.
 224: - Replay currently reads, writes, and performs the scoped run-owned purge on
 225:   shared `fix122_evaluation`; Section 4.2a defines the only allowed
 226:   transitional exception and its delete policy. Replay can also write
 227:   `shared_candle_input_audit` in shared-market LIVE mode.

 231: layer, static reachability checks, and direct/async rejection tests. The E01
 232: audit must also decide whether every shared-table write is replaced by a
 233: Replay-owned record or made unreachable in the baseline.

 394: 
 395: FIX-156 may change Replay-owned code, fixtures, isolated Replay tables,
 396: tests, and documentation only. It must not change Production trading logic,
 397: Production wallet behaviour, Production database records, live Binance
 398: behaviour, or deployment configuration.

 353:                         addPercent, open.positionPercent(), false, replayWalletSettings.getMaximumDailyNewPositions(),
 354:                         replayExecutedNewPositions, fresh.price());
 355:                 BigDecimal budget = sizing.spend();
 356:                 if (sizing.allowed()) {
 357:                     addPercent = sizing.normalizedPositionPercent();
 358:                     BigDecimal addedQty = sizing.quantity();
 359:                     persistBuy(runId, symbol, signal, fresh, addedQty, budget, addPercent, decision);
 360:                     executionIntelligenceService.markExecuted(signal, decision);
 361:                     // FIX-112A: a progressive add consumes only its own triggering signal.
 362:                     replayScope.markEntryConsumed(signal.getId());

 364:                     open = addToPosition(runId, open, signal, addedQty, budget, addPercent);

 366:             }
 367:         }
 368:         }

 370:         BigDecimal finalWallet = cash;
 371:         if (open != null && !timeline.isEmpty()) {
 372:             BigDecimal mark = timeline.get(timeline.size() - 1).getLatestPrice();
 373:             if (mark != null) finalWallet = finalWallet.add(mark.multiply(open.quantity(), MC), MC);
 374:         }

 382:         defensiveRiskReductionObserver.logRunSummary(runId, symbol);

 384:         // FIX-109: expose whether this run had the persisted live-price stream required

 392:     /**

 394:      * 1m live-price observation stream. The order mirrors LivePositionProtectionService:
 395:      * TAKE_PROFIT -> STOP_LOSS -> PROFIT_LOCK -> normal HTF exit. The newly closed
 396:      * 1m signal is intentionally not visible yet; BinanceKlineService calls onPrice()

 399:     private LivePriceEvaluation evaluateLivePrice(long runId, String symbol, ShadowPosition p,
 400:                                                    MarketPriceEventService.PriceEvent event,
 401:                                                    TradeSignal oneMinute, TradeSignal five, TradeSignal one) {
 402:         BigDecimal price = event.price();
 403:         ShadowPosition current = p;
 404: 
 405:         if (current.takeProfit() != null && price.compareTo(current.takeProfit()) >= 0) {
 406:             PositionContinuationPolicy.Evaluation continuation = continuationPolicy.evaluate(
 407:                     oneMinute, five, one,
 408:                     current.entryTrend(), current.entryStructure(), current.entryMomentum(), current.entryVolume(),
 409:                     current.entryConfidence(), current.entryScore());
 410:             if (continuation.extendTarget()) {
 411:                 BigDecimal distance = current.takeProfit().subtract(current.entryPrice());
 412:                 BigDecimal oldTarget = current.takeProfit();
 413:                 BigDecimal newTarget = oldTarget.add(distance.multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(0.50), MC), MC);
 414:                 ProfitLockState beforeState = current.profitLockState();
 415:                 current = rebaseForExtendedTarget(current, newTarget, price, event.observedAt()).withTakeProfit(newTarget);
 416:                 BigDecimal fix118NewProgress = progressPercent(current.entryPrice(), newTarget, current.highest());
 417:                 log.info("[FIX-118][REPLAY][TP_EXTENSION] runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, observedAt={}, marketPrice={}, " +
 418:                                 "entry={}, oldTp={}, newTp={}, highest={}, profitLockActive={}, profitLockPrice={}, previousState={}, state={}, " +
 419:                                 "newGeometryProgressPct={}",
 420:                         runId, current.positionId(), symbol, event.observedAt(), price, current.entryPrice(), oldTarget, newTarget,
 421:                         current.highest(), current.profitLockActive(), current.profitLockPrice(), beforeState, current.profitLockState(), fix118NewProgress);
 422:                 jdbcTemplate.update("UPDATE wallet_position_test SET take_profit_usdt=? WHERE id=?", newTarget, current.positionId());
 423:                 persistProfitLockState(current);
 424:                 persistNearTpState(current);
 425:                 persistManagementAtPrice(runId, symbol, event, "TAKE_PROFIT_EXTENDED", oldTarget, newTarget, current, continuation.explanation());
 426:                 return new LivePriceEvaluation(ExitDecision.hold(), current);
 427:             }
 428:             // FIX-11Q: baseline continuation has already failed. Only now may the replay-only
 429:             // counterfactual evaluate the one-candle grace. Existing PASS/TP-extension behavior above
 430:             // always wins first and is never intercepted by this experiment.
 431:             oneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver.observeBaselineTakeProfitFailure(
 432:                     runId, symbol, current.positionId(), event.observedAt(), event.price(),
 433:                     current.entryTime(), current.entryPrice(), current.quantity(), current.cost(),
 434:                     current.stopLoss(), current.takeProfit(), current.highest(),
 435:                     current.profitLockActive(), current.profitLockPrice(),
 436:                     current.entryScore(), current.entryConfidence(), current.entryTrend(), current.entryStructure(),
 437:                     current.entryMomentum(), current.entryVolume(), oneMinute, five, one, continuation.explanation());
 438:             persistManagementAtPrice(runId, symbol, event, "TAKE_PROFIT_EXIT", current.takeProfit(), current.takeProfit(), current, continuation.explanation());
 439:             return new LivePriceEvaluation(new ExitDecision(true, "TAKE_PROFIT", continuation.explanation()), current);
 440:         }
 441: 
 442:         // Production stop-loss has priority over profit-lock evaluation.
 443:         if (current.stopLoss() != null && price.compareTo(current.stopLoss()) <= 0) {
 444:             return new LivePriceEvaluation(new ExitDecision(true, "STOP_LOSS",
 445:                     "Live price " + price + " reached stop loss " + current.stopLoss()), current);
 446:         }
 447: 
 448:         ShadowPosition updated = profitLockState(current, price);
 449:         if (!equalsNullable(current.highest(), updated.highest())
 450:                 || current.profitLockActive() != updated.profitLockActive()
 451:                 || !equalsNullable(current.profitLockPrice(), updated.profitLockPrice())
 452:                 || current.profitLockState() != updated.profitLockState()
 453:                 || !java.util.Objects.equals(current.profitLockRebaseStartedAt(), updated.profitLockRebaseStartedAt())) {
 454:             jdbcTemplate.update("UPDATE wallet_position_test SET highest_price_usdt=?, profit_lock_active=?, profit_lock_price_usdt=?, profit_lock_state=?, profit_lock_rebase_started_at=? WHERE id=?",
 455:                     updated.highest(), updated.profitLockActive(), updated.profitLockPrice(), updated.profitLockState().name(),
 456:                     updated.profitLockRebaseStartedAt() == null ? null : Timestamp.from(updated.profitLockRebaseStartedAt()), updated.positionId());
 457:         }
 458:         if (updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE
 459:                 || updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 460:                 || !equalsNullable(current.highest(), updated.highest())
 461:                 || current.profitLockActive() != updated.profitLockActive()
 462:                 || !equalsNullable(current.profitLockPrice(), updated.profitLockPrice())
 463:                 || current.profitLockState() != updated.profitLockState()) {
 464:             BigDecimal fix118Progress = updated.takeProfit() == null || updated.takeProfit().compareTo(updated.entryPrice()) <= 0
 465:                     ? BigDecimal.ZERO
 466:                     : updated.highest().subtract(updated.entryPrice(), MC)
 467:                             .multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100), MC)
 468:                             .divide(updated.takeProfit().subtract(updated.entryPrice(), MC), 6, RoundingMode.HALF_UP);
 469:             log.info("[FIX-118][REPLAY][LIVE_PATH] runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, observedAt={}, price={}, entry={}, target={}, " +
 470:                             "highest={}, previousActive={}, active={}, previousState={}, state={}, previousLock={}, lock={}, progressPct={}, triggered={}",
 471:                     runId, updated.positionId(), symbol, event.observedAt(), price, updated.entryPrice(), updated.takeProfit(),
 472:                     updated.highest(), current.profitLockActive(), updated.profitLockActive(), current.profitLockState(), updated.profitLockState(),
 473:                     current.profitLockPrice(), updated.profitLockPrice(), fix118Progress,
 474:                     updated.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE && updated.profitLockPrice() != null
 475:                             && price.compareTo(updated.profitLockPrice()) <= 0);
 476:         }
 477:         current = updated;
 478: 
 479:         BigDecimal rebaseHardFloor = current.entryPrice().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(1.0005), MC);
 480:         if (current.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 481:                 && price.compareTo(rebaseHardFloor) < 0) {
 482:             String reason = "TP-extension rebase safety floor was breached at " + rebaseHardFloor + ". " + profitLockConfigText();
 483:             log.info("[FIX-118][REPLAY][REBASE_HARD_FLOOR] runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, observedAt={}, price={}, floor={}, target={}",
 484:                     runId, current.positionId(), symbol, event.observedAt(), price, rebaseHardFloor, current.takeProfit());
 485:             persistManagementAtPrice(runId, symbol, event, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", current.takeProfit(), current.takeProfit(), current, reason);
 486:             return new LivePriceEvaluation(new ExitDecision(true, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", reason), current);
 487:         }
 488: 
 489:         if (current.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE && current.profitLockPrice() != null
 490:                 && price.compareTo(current.profitLockPrice()) <= 0) {
 491:             log.info("[FIX-118][REPLAY][LIVE_TRIGGER] runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, observedAt={}, price={}, lock={}, target={}",
 492:                     runId, current.positionId(), symbol, event.observedAt(), price, current.profitLockPrice(), current.takeProfit());
 493:             BigDecimal hardProfitFloor = current.entryPrice().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(1.0005), MC);
 494:             if (price.compareTo(hardProfitFloor) < 0) {
 495:                 String reason = "Profit-lock hard floor was breached; protected profit can no longer be preserved. "
 496:                         + profitLockConfigText();
 497:                 persistManagementAtPrice(runId, symbol, event, "PROFIT_LOCK_HARD_EXIT", current.takeProfit(), current.takeProfit(), current, reason);

 499:             }

 501:             if (lockDecision.exit()) {
 502:                 String reason = "Price retraced to the protected profit level after a profitable advance. "
 503:                         + lockDecision.explanation() + " " + profitLockConfigText();
 504:                 persistManagementAtPrice(runId, symbol, event, "PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT", current.takeProfit(), current.takeProfit(), current, reason);
 505:                 return new LivePriceEvaluation(new ExitDecision(true, lockDecision.code(), reason), current);
 506:             }
 507:             persistManagementAtPrice(runId, symbol, event, "PROFIT_LOCK_HOLD", current.takeProfit(), current.takeProfit(), current,
 508:                     lockDecision.explanation() + " " + profitLockConfigText());
 509:         }
 510: 
 511:         PositionExitPolicy.Evaluation normalExit = exitPolicy.evaluateNormalExit(oneMinute, five, one);
 512:         if (normalExit.exit()) {
 513:             return new LivePriceEvaluation(new ExitDecision(true, normalExit.code(), normalExit.explanation()), current);
 514:         }
 515: 
 516:         // FIX-11T: shared Production/Replay rule, evaluated LAST after all existing exits.
 517:         NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State nearTpState = new NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State(
 518:                 current.nearTpState(), current.nearTpBestPrice(), current.nearTpBearishStreak(),
 519:                 current.nearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(), current.nearTpHarvestUsed());
 520:         NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.Evaluation nearTp = nearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.evaluate(
